[[11:28:12]] 1. Point Report of the Committee on Justice on the Government template (808 e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law establishing a federal law implementing the European Public Prosecutor's Office (EUSTA-DG) and amending the Law on Justice and Public Prosecutor's Service, the Federal Law on Judicial Cooperation in Criminal Matters with the Member States of the European Union, the Extradition and Legal Assistance Act, the Financial Penal Code and the Criminal Code (Strafrechts EU-Adaptation Act 2021 – StrEU-AG 2021) (859 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e7091	We come to item 1 of the agenda. I should like to extend my warmest welcome to the Federal Minister in our midst. Mr Stefan is the first to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e7118	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This item on the agenda is about the introduction of a European Public Prosecutor. That means: Outsourcing of justice and prosecution to the European Union, to a state association that does not have its own justice, no own revenue and no criminal offences. We are thus ridding ourselves of any influence, any control over this prosecutor. You have to look at what that means in principle. It is, in fact, a system breach that we commit, because prosecution by the prosecutor is a typical state task. It is the task of the National State. We are constantly discussing what responsibility there is, where the law of interconnection takes effect, and where democratic control is ultimately behind it. All of this is lifted if we introduce this European Public Prosecutor's Office. It is based on a regulation of the European Union, but it must be said that not all states participate in this European Public Prosecutor's Office. That means it was possible not to participate. Five European countries, namely Sweden, Ireland, Denmark, Poland and Hungary, do not take part in this European Public Prosecutor's Office for good reason, with the same arguments that we too oppose. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Before setting up such an institution, you have to think about what you do, what you do, what is better, and whether the nation state can actually better regulate things itself, whether you are misunderstandingd by Austrian justice, or whether Austrian justice is being discharged financially. To all these questions one can only say: obviously no! It is true that these criminal offences in Austria already have to be prosecuted. It is true that we are not relieved financially, but of course we have to pay it extra. It is also true that there is a very clear principle of which the EIA always speaks so much: the principle of subsidiarity. That is, what you can better regulate in the nation state, you have to, you should also regulate that in the nation state. That is to say, one deliberately rejects it because one has the ideological concept, one wants to disband the nation state and instead create a state. This is another step in this direction: One makes a European prosecutor, even though the word prosecutor already says that he belongs to a state, and in fact wants to break the system. The Committee has already said: We're writing story! – Yes, that says everything, that is, one makes it aware. Of course you can find this well, you can stand by it and say that you want a European state, you want to disband the nation states, but think It is only because of the way in which we have to act in a crisis! It shows how important it is that the nation state works. For example, especially in the Covid crisis, it has been seen that the European Union is not working, on the contrary, it is ultimately dependent on the fact that it works internally. What is this prosecution necessary for, what is it made for? – It is about crimes against the financial interests of the European Union. That sounds so nice now, but what does that mean? Is it not, in fact, to address any less revenue of any state? The European Union itself has no revenue, it lives on Member States' contributions. So if a Member State now has less revenue, this is, of course, an indirect financial matter for the European Union. That is to say, just as it is vaguely formulated and as we already know the European Union, competence will be expanded more and more. This is therefore extremely problematic, in other words a complete undermine of our sovereignty without any control, without any possibility of interoperability, in total, a completely false development. I can only plead for this: Also vote against, make Don't take this step in the wrong direction!

The criminal organizations have, of course, also managed to withdraw from the national investigation authorities. Ladies and gentlemen! It is really about very, very much money – a number has already been mentioned: EUR 1.2 billion – in the context of EU budget and promotion abuse. In 2018, for example, the amounts reported by the national authorities at the cost of the EU budget amounted to EUR 1,197 billion. The responsibilities were divided under the premise of the best possible protection of the EU budget. Good cross-border cooperation is in our best interests. Crime has many facets and is known to know no bounds. The final effect is that we protect the tax money of European citizens.

Fill up this hole and set motivational incentives – for example, a sommelier, who is reported in the city for short work, can still be animated for full-time work in the holiday hotel industry – is the great challenge. It is also important to provide incentives and, as you say correctly, to provide short-time work with other parameters. There are two different interests in the tourist area. One of them says: I want to keep my employees, so I keep them in short work. – But we have to solve the problem, because at present we do not solve any problem at all, but only prolong the situation. I think it is particularly important to be able to occupy these open spaces too: out of short work, into the balance of the shortage of skilled workers. [[Congratulations with the NEOS.]] Finally, I would like to say one more thing because the craft sector, the construction industry and these areas are also mentioned in the short work: We talked about the investment premium only yesterday, there is a real boom – I do not understand where black spots exist on the Austrian map, where there is no good order situation. I believe that there is also a need to put on: that full-time posts are especially attractively designed and, above all, the abuse – I emphasize with intention: abuse – is eliminated. This is a topic – it is not only to be seen in tourism, but in all the works. Wherever you go, people are still in short-time work. It was generous, but for 2020 it was enough to show itself generously. 2021, we need to be honest, we don't need this generosity anymore, and that just seems to be the follow-up.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e10664	Madam President. Estimated Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! For all of us in Austria, it is natural that if we visit a hospital at any time of day or night because of an accident or acute illness, we will immediately receive competent medical assistance. How is that possible? This is due to the so-called journal services. Our doctors – today's application refers essentially to the medical service – do their job at a regular day's work, and then some of them remain as a service team in the hospital and move into the so-called journal service, which then lasts until the next day in the morning. These journal services are basically designed in such a way that one is not always active in them, but is willing to take up work at any time when medical work is needed. Now this stress is quite different. It depends on the professional discipline, on the hospital location, on the service category and, of course, also on chance – once a lot is going on, once a little less – but every hour in the hospital, every hour presence counts as working time, and that is true. Since 2015, in Austria, EU standards have been applied that doctors in the hospital can be obliged to do such work and presence for up to 48 hours a week. This means – in order to imagine that – when someone does such a journal service on Monday and Thursday, this results in a total of 48 hours and actually already the average weekly working time. Now, however, the EU also allows doctors to voluntarily make an opt-out, which means a perhaps longer presence in the hospital. In Austria, up to 55 hours are currently possible, but only with an operating agreement and the individual voluntary and revocable consent of the individual doctor or doctor. Some of our colleagues do so, a larger part does not take this into account, but remains for 48 hours. What we are doing now is to extend this possibility for up to 55 hours once more by four years, and then this maximum possible time for another three years goes down to 52 hours. It is crucial to keep in mind that this is our system of solidarity, and when we think about how important it is, it becomes clear that we are making an extremely balanced proposal here, because it is not one-dimensional on one side or on the other side of the interest, but it is doing what we need socially, and that is good. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to say a very thank you very much to my medical professional colleagues who, by their flexibility in the work, ensure the round-the-clock care in health for all of us, and I would like to express my thanks to all those who work in the health professions who have always done a lot but have done a lot in recent months, all of them I would like to include in this thanks. - Thank you many times.

The parents submit a request to the text message, but the remaining costs of EUR 3 500 must be paid in advance in the text and the SMS must be accepted. Therefore, they submit a request to the ÖGK Support Fund. In August, the support fund of the ÖGK grants support of almost 1 000 euros. Many personal fates, many fears, many worries, hopelessness and shame are associated with them. The financial need often leads to chronic stress, which burdenes the body and psyche enormously. The longer the debt situation lasts, the worse physical and mental health also becomes. Ladies and gentlemen! The debt protection shield required in our request would be a comprehensive means of resolving the crisis situation of the people concerned, from the collection fees to bank accounts and financial education. It is a pity for the many people affected in Austria that our request will not find a majority today, as well as in the Committee.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e14627	We're going to the 14th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Prammer is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e14655	Mr President. Minister of Sports! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! The next men's World Cup will take place in Qatar. This is a country in which there is no equal rights, no freedom of expression, no freedom of religion, no freedom of the press, a country in which workers are exploited in a slave-like labour-law relationship. [[with fingers to indicate quotation marks]] It is good and it is right that there are now numerous initiatives to highlight this and to urge it and to try to change it. Some small successes have already been achieved through these campaigns, so that, for example, this exploitary Kabala system should be abolished. There have already been some amendments to the law, which – you hear and marvel! – allow for a self-determined leave or change of job for workers and guarantee regular payment. So it's really bad that you have to say something like that at all. At least that's what it's been doing now, but that's a very great achievement. This is just an example of many of how such sports events go to countries that actually do not deserve the positive effects of such sports events. Actually, it would have to be just the other way around. It is necessary that countries first produce these human rights standards and then can only be granted the events to these countries. In order to ensure that these tourist added value, these marketing added values, which are increased by increasing reporting, this increased use by the construction of stadiums, by the construction of accommodation establishments, by the whole redesign of cities and districts, by investing in public transport and road development, that all this benefits a country, one can really create an incentive, but first of all the minimum human rights standards must be established. It is important that we act as politicians to ensure that this can be done, because in principle it is the case that the sports associations, the international sports associations themselves determine in which countries they carry out their major events. But if in a country a football world championship, the Olympic Games or the Paralympic Games take place, that is a very, very great added value for this country. It is important that we, as a policy, create the necessary structures to ensure that the sports organisations align these awards according to these criteria. The sports associations are bound by the laws of their countries in the individual nations, and they can only act within these criteria, which policy lays down. For this reason, it is very, very important that these criteria for major sporting events be voted on internationally. It does not matter if individual countries submit criteria to their sports associations, since they are concerned with majority decisions in international associations and there the national associations are to be able to speak with a strong voice, with a strong voice, with a European voice. It is therefore important that we give them opportunities and provisions that are in line in the European environment. The current application is precisely concerned with and requires exactly that. I think it's a little a pity that there's some kind of competition here, who first came up with what idea. – Dear Max, yes, you have made a motion for a resolution last time, but before the NEOS had already tabled a request, and we too were already in the vote for an amendment. [[towards Abg. Köllner]] That means: What's bad about all of us working on the same subject when we work on the same interests, when we get together and do it together? This competition: I got first!, I got first!, No, I got first!, actually doesn't bring us here. – The fact is: We're doing this together now. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] By the way, as regards joint: We have decided unanimously in committee, I am delighted whether we will also decide unanimously here – I very much hope so. It is already an amendment in circulation, and I would like to make a brief comment on this. Unfortunately, there the human rights issue is remixed and mixed with the vaccine theme and, from my point of view, this really good request is a little valued. [[Abg. Steger:... Amendment request!]] - Please? - Okay, you're gonna make another request. [[Abg. Steger: This is not an amendment!]] I would nevertheless like to comment on this because: It has always been so, and it will always be the case that in the event of sporting events abroad, the entry conditions of the host countries must be complied with. It has always been that way and will always be so, because otherwise it will not work. Sports events are also quite normal in the context of the existing entry regulations. It has never been a problem that, if certain vaccinations have been planned in certain countries, these provisions must be complied with. This is so with hepatitis, that is with malaria – that is so with all vaccinations. If they have been prescribed for travel, these provisions must be complied with. It is also so with visa provisions that they must be respected. It is not an obligation, and this is not a restriction on human rights. Let us not bring this valuable proposal and this important issue to this level!

ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e15290	Mr President. Dear Mr. Vice-Chancellor – for the second time! Perhaps in the first place, because we discussed this last and there was also a parliamentary question about how you will be behaved in Qatar at a World Cup, I would like to ask the following question in the room. Imagine, the World Championship is not in Qatar, but, for example, in Saudi Arabia, in order to bring a particularly bad example, and imagine further, we did not consider you – which is not an underestimation, but rather into the future – but we have, for example, a female sports minister or we have a gay Federal Chancellor or Foreign Minister, and imagine these will be invited. How would the Federal Government actually behave there? If one belongs to the latter category, then there is the death penalty on that behavior. Human rights and their universal validity are one of the greatest achievements of liberal democracy, and through the Universal Declaration of Human Rights of the United Nations, they are important for the entire world population. The respect for human rights must therefore also be taken into account in sport and, in particular, in major sporting events. The award of large sports events is, of course, an unpolitical act in an ideal world, but authoritarian regimes have always used major sporting events to legitimise their power. You only have to look back in history: A particularly sharp example was 1936 when Nazi Germany played the Olympic Games in Germany in order to stage themselves accordingly on the world stage. So we must not close our eyes when totalitarian states misuse the sport to wash themselves in public at world level – and to deny that this is happening is naïve. As a sports speaker from NEOS, I am therefore very pleased that our initiative, our original request, which we made a few months ago, was taken up here. I would also like to thank Mr Sirkka Prammer, in particular the chairman of the Sports Committee, for the joint initiative that has come into being here and for the fact that we, as the Austrian Parliament, will then jointly position ourselves. The specific occasion for this request is Qatar, which has already been addressed, but also China, where the upcoming Winter Olympics will take place. And while in Qatar – and this has already been said by Mr Graf – after massive pressure from the media and NGOs’s improvements could be achieved, especially in the treatment of workers, the situation in China is repeated in the media due to human rights violations – and nothing has improved there at all. In China we are dealing with huge detention camps for the minority of the Uygurs and the continued repression of democracy movements, and the Chinese regime is also not aware that there are any changes planned. There is therefore a need for clear statements from the European Union, and certain minimum standards must finally be introduced in the event of large sporting events. countries, those who put their population in camps, countries that disregard human rights or restrict any form of freedom of expression, must not be venues for sporting events. And even if the decision is often with the relevant sports organisations or national committees, the elected representatives of liberal democracies, as we are here, should have a vehement and repeated influence on these organisations that make decisions. We know on a very recent occasion that sports and politics are often closer to each other than they are right. At this point also from our side a warm congratulations to colleagues Schmidhofer for the election to the ÖSV President. I wish you all the best! I think that's gonna happen. You also need to modernize this ski association and bring in fresh wind, which has been painfully missing in the last decades. [[Cheerfulness of the Abg. Riser]] So, what's this about? – New procurement directives are intended to comply with certain minimum standards in the process of searching for future host countries. It is now up to the Federal Government, in particular to you, Mr Sport Minister, and to the Foreign Minister, to work to achieve these objectives consistently and ambitiously at European level, and we will closely monitor your international and European efforts in this regard.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-05-20-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00107_d8e15369	Mr President. Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen. You can't repeat it often enough: It is true that Qatar is stepping up human rights and the rights of workers with feet. Thousands of them have died while they have built up the stadiums for the World Cup, and that is why all of us are concerned. All those who will sit in front of the television must be aware of this, and that is why we are also holding this debate in the Austrian Parliament today. [[As a result of this, the European Parliament has adopted a number of amendments to the Treaty establishing the European Economic Community, including those tabled by the European Parliament and the Council. Fireplaces.]] China – and that has already been said – suppresses minorities, like all those who do not conform exactly to the behaviour of the country’s autocratic leadership, and the world must not ignore it if a cheerful spectacle of sports will take place next year. The debate on sport and politics and human rights is very up-to-date, but is already very old. At the heart of the issue is what sport has to do with human rights. Perhaps you will remember or have read about the fact that already during the 1936 Summer Olympics, during the Hitler dictatorship, the athletes and athletes in the USA have argued about whether they should go to Berlin at all. In the end, they drove. On the one hand, they helped the National Socialists to polish up their reputation in the world, but on the other hand, it was not uninteresting to observe how the racist Nazi regime responded when a non-white US light athlete won four gold medals in his own stadium, and thus more than any other. This makes it clear that sport, human rights and politics are inseparably interwoven. We must therefore also benefit from the high social importance of sport and also the role model of sport in order to address human rights. And yes, if we ask ourselves today: Allow athletes, sportsmen in states where human rights are be trodden with feet, fight for medals at all?, then we can only answer morally and ethically: No, no, no, You, the officials, the politicians from democratic states, should not sit on honorary stands together with autocrats. They should not appear in international associations, in states filling their pockets with dirty money. But, of course, it is also a matter of discussing the whole thing in advance, and this is precisely what the proposal is aimed at. In principle, however, I believe – and this is the second side of the medal – that sports can overcome political, religious and cultural boundaries and in practice this question, boycott yes or no, is not so easy to answer. As the example of Qatar has shown, international pressure can cause something to change. There were the minimum wages, for example, there was an exchange of athletes, sportsmen with the host workers themselves, and it will also be no different for China than for this international pressure to be built up and also to be held high on our part. In other words, the debate on the field of tension between sport, politics and human rights has a value, has added value. No human would talk about the situation of the workers today if we did not look at it if there were not an international component in it. And yes, it is a little hope, but I assume that the Chinese state will not be able to trample around in the spotlight of the international public in the near future. It's not much, but it's not much. The most important thing in the current debate is probably the debate itself. It contributes to the fact that we look a little more closely and that we make it visible that human rights are also indivisible in sport. Thank you.

It is an adaptation of the existing fertiliser law to EU law and regulates the marketing, labelling and control, not the implementation and not the use – which fall within the competence of the countries. Criteria such as resource efficiency, sustainability, soil and environmental protection are taken into account as part of the authorisations. Fertilizers containing pollutants must not be placed on the market. [[Rejoicing with the greens.]] I would probably have taken it over – but I would have had it certified. What it's about me: If one grows up as much as I do, or on a farm or cattle farm, one cannot really imagine the reality of farms, pig farming, pig fattening. I have looked at this more and more closely last year, and I have noticed that there are two realities. There is a reality that applies to producers – and a reality that applies to consumers, as I am. I am buying organic meat in principle, and this is directly from the farm. So how do you bring together two worlds as a politician, of which you know that there is such a gap? – This is not going from today to tomorrow, I am also aware of it as a greener – but it is clear that we now have to set our first course. Yes, this request sets out for the first time that our aim is to get out of genetically modified feed, and that is a milestone. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] This request also states that we will further develop the animal welfare criteria for the AMA seal, which is also a milestone. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Double farmer.]] What does that mean? – This means that we get away from the fact that Austria continues to import genetically modified soya that is grown on areas where rainforests used to stand. We're getting away from the fact that we're heating up CO2 emissions. We get away from the fact that we import 500 000 tonnes of soya from overseas each year. That won't go from today to tomorrow, and yes, I'm convinced: The colleagues of the opposition parties will ensure that we continue with the ÖVP on the necessary parking screws. We will continue to do so, because the industry needs it. Now I come back to these two divergent worlds. [[Abg. The animal must be raised, it must be maintained, it must be fed until it goes to its fate. For this you get 140, 150 euros as a farmer – and a pig schnitzel costs less than cat feed in the supermarket, these are the different realities, ladies and gentlemen! But the AMA does not talk about it, nobody speaks about it in advertising – nor does our Mrs Agriculture Ministers. It always complains that everything costs so little, but in the end it persistently refuses to change the system. The fact is that Austria imports about 500 000 tonnes of soya in the year. This is soy that those animals are given as feed, which then receive the red-white-red AMA seal. Of course, consumers do not know what that means: They do not know that these products contain genetically modified soya. The farmers know it, the politicians know it, and the trade knows, of course, too – but nothing happens. But what do we want NEOS? – We want to show NEOS to consumers: Hey, it's not that simple – when you buy meat with AMA seal, it says that this animal has been fed with soya from South America! What does that mean? – That the soya has changed genetically is the first point. The second point is that this soya has been grown in such a way that sprays have been used that are no longer permitted in Europe. The third point – and that is actually the worst thing for me personally – is that illegal rainforest has been burned to grow this food for the hungry Europe that wants to be fed. We want regionally produced products, with soybean products from Europe, to have a higher appreciation because no rainforest has to be wooded for them. That is what consumers need to understand, that is the transparency that belongs to this system. However, we also want to help farmers generate more income without hanging on these miserable funding pots. If the consumer understands that there is a higher value, he will also be ready – as with the eggs – to pay a higher price. We also want this miserable backward agricultural policy in Austria to be finally broken down. It is always suggested that everything would be so great and so great, and that is simply not true! It needs movement, it needs transparency, it needs a further development of this agricultural system, otherwise there will be no agriculture in Austria in 20, 30, 40 years. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, I believe that what happened in the Committee on Agriculture was actually a bit of a joy. I believe that practitioners here in the hall already know that the system needs to develop, that something new belongs. I think it would be necessary to get support for my request, which is so clear, so simple and so easy to understand. Ries.]] This brings back to this debate, because it is a little more complex in the Commission's causa, and I would like to explain very briefly why it is more complex. Of course, we need to discuss the precise interpretation of the extension of the duty of truth, since this amendment concerns not only us as a Parliament but, of course, also the informers – and that is the point. As already mentioned, the Committee of Inquiry has a duty of truth, but this committee is also very special: On the one hand, the information person has the opportunity to take and bring a trusted person, but on the other hand, there is also a lawyer and a procedural judge who is in charge of protection – and that is important! – the rights of the informers – this is important: obliged! – are. This is also a good thing, and it is also a cornerstone of the Rules of Procedure of the Committee of Inquiry. If we were to introduce the obligation of truth into a normal committee, just as the Standing Subcommittee of the Court of Auditors is just one, these would be missing. That is to say, we need to discuss the issue of the protection of the rights of the data subject more widely – and we need to discuss it widely before such an amendment is made. We also need to discuss the actual demand for cargo, the charging practice in committees, because this duty of truth would, of course, also affect all the persons concerned. There is a differentiation between the rules between ministers, as is the case in committees, and only those available to the Committee, which in principle is only available to the Committee. Only ministers may pass on questions on to their cabinet staff, as opposed to persons, officials, external persons who are invited to the committee – they must not pass on these questions; only ministers may do so. This is regulated in the Rules of Procedure of the National Council – there is a difference. This is also a bit unfair because ministers get out of there better than people who are then externally loaded. You see the complexity in this whole causa. It is also necessary to consider how this can be reconciled with basic procedural principles such as the right to a fair procedure and the right of withdrawal of accused persons or a prohibition of self-discharge. Think of Hygiene Austria! If we have people here who are accused in proceedings, how do they turn out? This is not regulated by a Rules of Procedure. There are some questions there where, on the one hand, the desire for truth and on the other, the protection of the rights of the persons concerned must be met. If these concerns are raised enough – and I consider them to be very useful – we Greens see the extension of the duty of truth being extended beyond the committee of inquiry as useful, because we are in favour of the state working as transparently as possible – especially Parliament! – in order to provide citizens with the necessary insights into our daily parliamentary work. Thank you.
